- 2nd place (-0.34): 2-state with GoToColor black floor and GoAwayColor yellow - simpler foraging loop.
- 3rd (-0.07): Simple exploration works a little.
- Most others scored 0.00 or positive.

**Key insight for foraging:**
- We need robots to go to the GOOD source (one color), pick up items, then return to the nest (white floor, red wall).
- Avoid the BAD source (the other color that increases score).
- The champion uses: GoToColor(magenta clr=5) → transition to GoAwayColor(yellow clr=4) → Exploration → GoToColor(red clr=1, the nest wall).
- State 3 (GoToColor red=1) transitions back on gray floor or seeing green (clr=2).

**Strategy:**
1. **Survival**: Copy champion exactly.
2. **Exploitation** (~6 machines): Small parameter tweaks to champion topology.
3. **Exploration** (~7 machines): Different topologies focusing on the foraging loop.

For the foraging task, the key behaviors are:
- Navigate to source (black floor) 
- Avoid bad half (go toward blue or green walls)
- Return to nest (white floor, red wall)

Let me design machines with clearer foraging logic:
- State 0: Explore/go to black floor (source)
- State 1: Once on black, if near good items go to nest (white floor)
- State 2: Return - go toward red wall

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 2:**
- Champion (-1.41): 2-state FSM: GoToColor(yellow/clr=4) with transitions on gray floor, random prob, and neighbor conditions → GoAwayColor(yellow/clr=4) with transitions back on gray or random.
- The champion is surprisingly simple: go toward yellow things (the yellow wall block behind the good source?), then go away from yellow. This creates a foraging oscillation.
- 2nd/3rd (-0.84): The 4-state variants from round 1 also perform well.
- Big gap between rank 6 (-0.45) and rank 7 (-0.02) - machines 1-6 are clearly better.

**Key insight:** The champion uses clr=4 (yellow) for BOTH states - GoToColor(yellow) and GoAwayColor(yellow). This means:
- State 0: Go toward yellow-walled source area
- When enough neighbors (crowded at source) or random → switch to GoAwayColor(yellow), heading toward nest
- State 1: Go away from yellow (toward nest/white area)
- When on gray floor or random → return to State 0

This is a clean foraging loop! The yellow walls are behind one half of the source. The robot goes to the yellow source, picks up an item, then goes away from yellow (toward nest), delivers, then returns.

**Strategy:**
1. Copy champion exactly
2. Exploit champion: vary probabilities slightly, neighbor thresholds
3. Exploit champion: try different colors (blue=3, green=2) for source
4. Explore: 3-state versions with explicit nest detection (white floor)
5. Explore: combining the 2-state champion concept with a 3rd state for nest

The machines scoring 0.02-0.45 also show promise - the 3-state GoToColor(magenta)/GoAwayColor(yellow)/Explore with GoToColor(red) topology.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 3:**
- Champion (-7.61): 2-state, GoToColor(green/clr=2) + GoAwayColor(green/clr=2). Green = one half of the source area! This is huge - clr=2 targets the GREEN wall, which is the GOOD source (items that decrease score). The robot goes to the green source, picks up good items, then goes away from green (toward nest), delivers.
- 2nd (-1.78): Same topology but with clr=4 (yellow).
- 14th (15.85): clr=3 (blue) = BAD source! Going toward blue increases score. Confirmed: green is good, blue is bad.
- clr=1 (red) at rank 12 (0.00): going toward red (nest wall) from source doesn't help much.

**Key insight:** GREEN (clr=2) walls are behind the GOOD source half. The champion uses GoToColor(green) to find the good source, then GoAwayColor(green) to return to nest. This is elegant foraging.

**Strategy for Round 4:**
1. Copy champion exactly
2. Exploit: tune the champion's transition probabilities
3. Exploit: add a 3rd state for explicit nest detection (white floor) 
4. Exploit: try different neighbor thresholds in champion
5. Explore: 3-state with green source + red nest navigation
6. Explore: vary the return state (GoAwayColor green vs GoToColor white/red)

The champion's transitions:
- State 0 (GoToColor green): go to gray (p=0.14), random (p=0.22), neighbors>7 (p sigmoid w=12.7), neighbors>6 (p sigmoid w=19.07) → stay in state 0 (all self-loops... wait, destination is 0 which is "the other state" with 2 states!)

Wait - with 2 states, `--n0x0 0` means destination index 0 among OTHER states, so it points to state 1. All transitions from state 0 go to state 1. Similarly state 1 transitions go to state 0. This is correct - it's a 2-state oscillation.

State 0 → State 1 when: gray floor (p=0.14), random (p=0.22), many neighbors (>7 or >6)
State 1 → State 0 when: gray floor (p=0.95), random (p=0.4)

So state 0 (GoToColor green) switches when: on gray (transitioning through), or crowded (at source), or random.
State 1 (GoAwayColor green) switches back when: on gray (returning through middle) or random.

**Exploitation ideas:**
- Adjust p for gray in state 0 (currently 0.14 - low, keeps robots at source)
- Adjust p for gray in state 1 (currently 0.95 - high, quickly returns to source search)
- Try adding white floor detection in state 1 (nest arrival)
- Try 3-state: add explicit "at nest" state

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 4:**
- Champion (-8.33): State 0: GoToColor(green), transitions on BLACK floor (p=0.5), random (p=0.22), neighbors>7 (sigmoid), neighbors>6 (sigmoid) → State 1. State 1: GoAwayColor(green), transitions on WHITE floor (p=0.9), random (p=0.4) → State 0.
- Key insight: The champion uses BLACK floor (C=0, p=0.5) to trigger transition from GoToColor(green) to GoAwayColor(green). When the robot reaches the black floor (source area), it switches to returning! And WHITE floor (C=2, p=0.9) to switch back when it reaches the nest.
- 2nd (-8.00): Uses GRAY floor (C=1) to trigger switch from source, and lower neighbor thresholds (5,4). Very close!
- The 2-state GoToColor(green)/GoAwayColor(green) topology dominates. 3/4-state variants are worse.

**Key parameters to optimize:**
- State 0 transitions: black floor prob (0.5 in champion), random prob (0.22), neighbor thresholds
- State 1 transitions: white floor prob (0.9 in champion), random prob (0.4)
- The black floor trigger (p=0.5) in state 0 is crucial - when robot is ON the black source, it picks up item and immediately starts returning
- White floor trigger (p=0.9) in state 1 is crucial - when robot reaches nest, it quickly switches back

**Exploitation:** Vary p for black floor (0.3-0.8), white floor (0.7-1.0), random probs, neighbor thresholds.

**Exploration:** Try adding floor colors as additional triggers, or slightly different topologies.

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 5:**
- Champion (-9.07): p(black)=0.7 in state 0. The population is very tight (-9.07 to -6.03) with the 2-state GoToColor(green)/GoAwayColor(green) topology dominating.
- Key parameters: p(black floor) in state 0 ranges from 0.4-0.9, with 0.7 winning. p(white floor) in state 1 at 0.9 works well.
- Neighbor thresholds: the champion uses (7, w=12.7) and (6, w=19.07). 2nd place uses lower (5, w=10) and (4, w=15) - also good.
- The 4-transition state 0 structure (black floor + random + 2x neighbor) is consistently the best.
- State 1 with 2 transitions (white floor + random) is best - adding extra transitions hurts (rank 14).

**Fine-tuning focus:**
- p(black floor) in state 0: try 0.6, 0.75, 0.8, 0.85
- p(white floor) in state 1: try 0.85, 0.92, 0.95, 1.0  
- Random prob in state 0: try 0.18, 0.20, 0.25
- Random prob in state 1: try 0.3, 0.35, 0.45, 0.5
- Neighbor thresholds: try combinations of (5,10)+(4,15) and (6,12)+(5,18)
- Also try adding 1 more transition to state 1 (white floor + random + 1 neighbor check)

**Key insight:** The champion uses p(black)=0.7 - higher than previous 0.5. Maybe even higher (0.8, 0.9) is better since we want to reliably trigger the return trip when on the source. But 0.9 got -8.83 vs 0.7 getting -9.07, so there's a sweet spot around 0.7.

Let me try: 0.65, 0.72, 0.75, 0.80 for p(black), and also vary neighbor thresholds more.

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 6:**
- Champion (-9.07) still holds from round 5. All round 6 machines scored worse (-6.67 to -8.76).
- The topology is extremely stable: 2-state GoToColor(green)/GoAwayColor(green) with 4 transitions in state 0, 2 in state 1.
- Best this round: p(black)=0.7, neighbor thresholds (6,w=11)+(5,w=17) scored -8.76.
- The champion uses (7,w=12.7)+(6,w=19.07) - slightly higher thresholds.
- All machines are very close in score (within ~2 points). We're in a narrow local optimum.

**Key observations:**
- p(black floor) = 0.7 is consistently best
- p(white floor) = 0.9 in state 1 works well
- Random prob in state 0 = 0.22 is good; 0.25 also works; 0.20 is worse
- Neighbor thresholds in state 0: the champion's (7,12.7)+(6,19.07) is best so far
- Adding extra transitions to state 1 hurts performance (rank 13 this round)
- The scores are all very similar - we're near a local optimum

**Strategy for Round 7:**
- Keep champion exact
- Try fine-grained variations around p(black)=0.7: 0.68, 0.72, 0.73
- Try varying neighbor thresholds more: (7,15)+(6,20), (8,13)+(7,18), (6,12)+(5,17)
- Try p(white)=0.88, 0.93, 0.95 in state 1
- Try random=0.23, 0.24 in state 0
- Explore: combine best parameters differently
- Some genuine exploration: try 3-state with explicit nest detection built on the champion structure

The champion's exact parameters that work: p(black)=0.7, p(random)=0.22, thresh(7,w=12.73)+thresh(6,w=19.07), p(white)=0.9, p(random)=0.4.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 9 (Final Round):**
- Champion (-9.42) still holds from round 7. No improvement in rounds 8 or 9.
- Best this round: -9.33 (p_random=0.24). Second: -9.12 (3-transition state 1 with tiny black floor prob=0.05).
- The scores are noisy - the same machine (champion params) scored -8.29 in round 9 but -9.42 in round 7. High variance.
- Key insight: p(random_s0) = 0.23-0.24 is the sweet spot. 0.225 and 0.235 are worse.
- Adding a tiny black floor trigger (p=0.05) to state 1 scored -9.12 - interesting! When robot is at source going away from green, if it lands on black (still at source), it goes back to state 0 which re-approaches green.
- p(black_s0)=0.7 and p(white_s1)=0.9 remain optimal.

**Final round strategy:**
Since this is the last round, I should:
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Focus on the most promising variants found across all rounds
3. Try the 3-transition state 1 with black floor trigger at various probabilities (0.03, 0.05, 0.07, 0.10)
4. Try p(random)=0.23 + p(random)=0.24 combined with slight variations
5. Try combining p_random=0.24 (best this round) with the 3-transition state 1
6. A few wild explorations in case we're missing something

The noise level is about ±0.5, so differences smaller than that are not meaningful. Focus on robust variants.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

The arena contains a nest area at one end, with a WHITE floor, and a source area at the opposite end, with a BLACK floor. The floor in between is GRAY. The wall on the nest side is RED.

The source area is divided into two halves. The wall blocks behind one half are BLUE and those behind the other half are GREEN.

A robot carries at most one item. Entering a half of the source gives the robot an item of that half's type, replacing any item it was already carrying. Entering the nest with an item delivers it: an item from one half decreases the score by 1, an item from the other half increases it by 1.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
